Only private memory or regulated co-runners bound that multiplier.
  • Couplings act on four timescales around a millisecond tick: Rail droop acts in nanoseconds and interconnect queueing in microseconds, below the millisecond tick they threaten. Silicon thermal throttling, by contrast, persists for seconds after the load stops, and chassis structural and battery pack self-heating accumulates over multi-hour shifts. In the rejected shared-die placement, a halved clock stretches the enforcer’s 250 μs cycle to 500 μs while inference runs and for at least 2,175 ticks after it stops.
  • The declared bound is the falsification threshold: The declared worst-case execution time bounds the tick in isolation, and the selected placement claims that proposal-side load adds no interference term, so any loaded cycle above it refutes the placement’s timing premise even when the deadline still holds. A passing trace supports only the states it tested.
  • A refuted claim forces a priced choice: Regulation and reservation cost idle capacity, coloring and partitioning cost working set, workload reduction costs policy authority, and relocation adds a board link. On the machine that link costs 1.31 ms of a 63.3 ms dispatch-to-admission allocation dominated by inference and validation.
  • Platform exposure is a placement constraint: An on-die safety island with its own crystal, rail, and memory removes every die-level coupling except temperature, the reset chain, and the fabric its proposals cross. As of 2026, however, the commercial modules that carry the machine’s application processor do not enable it, so the permission path moves to a discrete controller.
  • The placement record bounds the deployment envelope: It takes its declared worst-case execution time and deadline from the enforcement record and adds loaded observed maxima, thermal headroom, bus arbitration, mitigations, and untested conditions. On the mobile manipulator the measurement fields are still empty, so the independence claim reaches release review without loaded qualification evidence.

Placement is where the third law becomes a property of hardware. Proposal is not permission only while the proposer cannot reach the permission decision through anything the two share, and on a real machine that includes memory, supply, clock, reset, and heat. The useful habit is to read every architecture diagram as a list of claims, one per shared resource, and to ask of each one what loaded measurement would refute it. The running machine leaves Part III with that placement decision and an explicit evidence gap: the record names the resources that must preserve the permission deadline, the measurements that could refute their bounds, and the fallback that must remain available if the proposer fails. Governing the machine means judging such conditional claims, and the next proposer to claim authority will be a person.

13.11 Historical Perspective and Further Reading

Avionics met the placement problem first. Once integrated modular avionics put functions of different criticality on one processor, the certifying question became whether a fault or overrun in one function could reach another. In Rushby (1999), the answer separates into two obligations: spatial partitioning, which protects memory and devices, and temporal partitioning, which protects the time each function is promised. The ARINC 653 interface turned the temporal half into fixed windows of a repeating frame, and Kopetz (2011) built whole architectures on a global time base so that timing could be designed rather than discovered. Automotive practice later wrote the same concern into ISO 26262 as freedom from interference and dependent-failure analysis (International Organization for Standardization 2018).

International Organization for Standardization. 2018. ISO 26262:2018: Road Vehicles, Functional Safety. International Organization for Standardization.
Puschner, Peter, and Christian Koza. 1989. “Calculating the Maximum Execution Time of Real-Time Programs.” Real-Time Systems 1 (2): 159–76. https://doi.org/10.1007/BF00571421.
Cousot, Patrick, and Radhia Cousot. 1977. “Abstract Interpretation: A Unified Lattice Model for Static Analysis of Programs by Construction or Approximation of Fixpoints.” Proceedings of the 4th ACM SIGACT-SIGPLAN Symposium on Principles of Programming Languages (POPL), 238–52. https://doi.org/10.1145/512950.512973.
Li, Yau-Tsun Steven, and Sharad Malik. 1995. “Performance Analysis of Embedded Software Using Implicit Path Enumeration.” Proceedings of the 32nd ACM/IEEE Design Automation Conference (DAC), 456–61. https://doi.org/10.1145/217474.217570.
Vestal, Steve. 2007. “Preemptive Scheduling of Multi-Criticality Systems with Varying Degrees of Execution Time Assurance.” IEEE International Real-Time Systems Symposium (RTSS), 239–43. https://doi.org/10.1109/RTSS.2007.47.

The bound itself had to be computed before it could be defended. Puschner and Koza (1989) showed how to derive a maximum execution time from program structure. Abstract interpretation (Cousot and Cousot 1977) gave analyzers a sound way to model caches and pipelines, and implicit path enumeration (Li and Malik 1995) turned the longest feasible path into an integer linear program. Wilhelm et al. (2008) surveys the tools that combined them, while Lundqvist and Stenström (1999) showed that local worst cases need not compose into a global one. Vestal (2007) then gave each task a budget per assurance level, the idea that lets a proposer overrun while an enforcer may not (Burns and Davis 2017).

Multicore processors undid the single-core premise. Cores that share caches and memory controllers turn the memory wall (Wulf and McKee 1995) from a throughput limit into a deadline problem. The software answers followed in quick succession: phased execution that confines memory access to a separate phase (Pellizzoni et al. 2011), per-core bandwidth regulation (Yun et al. 2013), and bank-aware allocation (Yun et al. 2014). Measurements then showed that partitioning capacity leaves the miss-handling structures shared (Valsan et al. 2016; Bechtel and Yun 2019), and certification caught up with AC 20-193 (Federal Aviation Administration 2024). What this chapter adds is the physical side of the argument. The enforcer’s bound is charged against a stopping distance, heat and supply couple the paths on timescales no scheduler sees, and the evidence travels to release in a placement record.

Wulf, Wm. A., and Sally A. McKee. 1995. “Hitting the Memory Wall: Implications of the Obvious.” ACM SIGARCH Computer Architecture News 23 (1): 20–24. https://doi.org/10.1145/216585.216588.
Pellizzoni, Rodolfo, Emiliano Betti, Stanley Bak, Gang Yao, John Criswell, Marco Caccamo, and Russell Kegley. 2011. “A Predictable Execution Model for COTS-Based Embedded Systems.” IEEE Real-Time and Embedded Technology and Applications Symposium (RTAS), 269–79. https://doi.org/10.1109/RTAS.2011.33.
Yun, Heechul, Renato Mancuso, Zheng-Pei Wu, and Rodolfo Pellizzoni. 2014. “PALLOC: DRAM Bank-Aware Memory Allocator for Performance Isolation on Multicore Platforms.” IEEE Real-Time and Embedded Technology and Applications Symposium (RTAS), 155–66. https://doi.org/10.1109/RTAS.2014.6925999.
Valsan, Prathap Kumar, Heechul Yun, and Farzad Farshchi. 2016. “Taming Non-Blocking Caches to Improve Isolation in Multicore Real-Time Systems.” IEEE Real-Time and Embedded Technology and Applications Symposium (RTAS), 1–12. https://doi.org/10.1109/RTAS.2016.7461361.
Bechtel, Michael G., and Heechul Yun. 2019. “Denial-of-Service Attacks on Shared Cache in Multicore: Analysis and Prevention.” 2019 IEEE Real-Time and Embedded Technology and Applications Symposium (RTAS), 357–67. https://doi.org/10.1109/RTAS.2019.00037.

For further reading, six works trace the ideas this chapter composes.

  • Rushby (1999) states the requirements for partitioning processors shared by functions of different criticality and separates the spatial and temporal obligations that the dependent-failure inventory of table 1 checks row by row.
  • Kopetz (2011) develops real-time system design from a global time base, the discipline behind fixed ticks, deadlines, and time-triggered exchange.
  • Wilhelm et al. (2008) surveys static, measurement-based, and hybrid methods for worst-case execution time and the premises each rests on, the comparison behind section 13.7.
  • Burns and Davis (2017) reviews mixed-criticality scheduling from its origin to multicore platforms, the theory behind letting the proposer lose renewal while the permission tick survives.
  • Yun et al. (2013) introduces per-core memory-bandwidth regulation, the mechanism that turns an unbounded interference multiplier on a shared die into a bounded one.
  • Federal Aviation Administration (2024) is the current certification guidance for multicore processors, and it shows what a regulator requires before a shared-die timing bound can be believed.
Kopetz, Hermann. 2011. Real-Time Systems: Design Principles for Distributed Embedded Applications. 2nd ed. Real-Time Systems Series. Springer. https://doi.org/10.1007/978-1-4419-8237-7.
Burns, Alan, and Robert I. Davis. 2017. “A Survey of Research into Mixed Criticality Systems.” ACM Computing Surveys 50 (6): 1–37. https://doi.org/10.1145/3131347.
Yun, Heechul, Gang Yao, Rodolfo Pellizzoni, Marco Caccamo, and Lui Sha. 2013. “MemGuard: Memory Bandwidth Reservation System for Efficient Performance Isolation in Multi-Core Platforms.” IEEE Real-Time and Embedded Technology and Applications Symposium (RTAS), 55–64. https://doi.org/10.1109/RTAS.2013.6531079.

What’s Next: From compute placement to intervention
When the slowest response delay belongs to a human operator, the stopping budget faces an interval that no silicon placement can bound. Every delay examined in this chapter belonged to the machine (a queued read, a stretched gate, a throttled clock), and each can be bounded or refuted by an empirical test on the chassis. Supervisory Intervention opens Part IV by addressing delays that originate outside the machine. A remote assistant taking over the mobile manipulator in an aisle introduces human reaction time over a network link that the onboard permission path cannot control, spending clearance from the exact same stopping budget. The authority to propose motion must then pass safely between the learned policy and the human operator while the permission path continues to validate every commanded actuation. That handover must never induce the hazardous motion it was intended to prevent, and every control tick must immutably record which entity held proposal authority.
